1. # **Use-case Model** {#use-case-model}

![][image1]  
The use-case model shows how users interact with PhysiX. Each use case corresponds to one feature in the PhysiX Vision document.  
**Actors**

* **User**: a Student or Physics Teacher using the same laboratory UI. Teachers use these experiment workflows for demonstrations; no separate teacher portal is implied.

* **AI Service:** an external system that generates the answers of the AI lab assistant. It does not calculate or change any measured value.

**Relationships**

* **Generalization between actors:** Student and Physics Teacher specialize User and inherit User’s use-case associations. Both roles use the same experiment UI.  
* **Use-case organization:** UC03 and UC03.1–UC03.17 are document groups, not executable use cases. Each experiment group contains four separate use cases: UC03.n.1 View introduction, UC03.n.2 Perform the Guided experiment, UC03.n.3 Perform the experiment in Open Lab, and UC03.n.4 View and compare results. These grouping relationships do not represent UML generalization.  
* **Include:** The former inclusion of shared UC04 is removed. Measurement, recording and comparison behavior is specified in each experiment’s Guided and Results use cases. Navigation between these use cases or access to shared trial data does not automatically establish an «include» relationship.  
* **Association:** User participates in UC01, UC02, UC05, UC07, UC08, UC09 and all four experiment-specific use cases in each group. AI Service participates in UC05 as a supporting actor.  
* **Access constraint:** UC03.n.3 Open Lab requires the same account to have successfully completed UC03.n.2 Guided for that experiment at least once. This is an access condition, not an «include» or generalization relationship.

**Use cases and product features**

* UC01 Sign up and sign in – Feature 1 User Accounts  
* UC02 Browse and search experiments, together with UC03.n.1 View experiment introduction – Feature 2 Experiment Library  
* UC03.n.2 Perform the Guided experiment – Feature 3 Guided Experiments  
* UC03.n.4 View and compare experiment results – Feature 4 Measurements and Results  
* UC05 Ask the AI lab assistant – Feature 5 AI Lab Assistant  
* UC03.n.3 Perform the experiment in Open Lab – Feature 6 Open Lab  
* UC07 View experiment history – Feature 7 Experiment History  
* UC08 Export experiment results – Feature 8 Export Results  
* UC09 Report a content error – Feature 9 Report a Content Error


2. # **Use-case Specifications** {#use-case-specifications}

   1. ## **Use-case: UC01 Sign up and sign in** {#use-case:-uc01-sign-up-and-sign-in}

| Use case Name | UC01 – Sign up and sign in |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | This use case describes how a user signs in to PhysiX with an email address and password, or creates a new account, so that their experiment results are saved, their experiment history can be viewed and content errors can be reported. It also covers resetting a forgotten password and signing out. Account requirements are defined in each use case. Access to experiment history, content-error reporting and unlocked Open Lab requires a signed-in account |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | On any screen, the user clicks the ‘Sign in’ button in the top-right corner. The system displays the Sign in form with the fields ‘Email’ and ‘Password’, the button ‘Sign in’, and the links ‘Create an account’ and ‘Forgot password?’. The user enters their email address and password and clicks ‘Sign in’. The system checks that the email address and password match a registered account. The system signs the user in, closes the form and returns the user to the screen they were on. An experiment in progress is not interrupted. The system displays the user’s name in the top-right corner in place of the ‘Sign in’ button. Clicking the name opens a menu with ‘History’ and ‘Sign out’. If an experiment is open and the user has already recorded trials, the system saves those trials to the user’s account. |
| Alternative Flows | **Alternative flow 1: The user does not have an account** From step 2 of the basic flow, the user clicks ‘Create an account’. The system displays the Sign up form with the fields ‘Full name’, ‘Email’, ‘Password’, ‘Confirm password’ and ‘Grade’ (a list with Grade 6 to Grade 12 and ‘Teacher’), the check box ‘I agree to the Terms of Use and Privacy Policy’, and the button ‘Create account’. The user fills in all fields, ticks the check box and clicks ‘Create account’. The system checks that every field is filled in, the email address has a valid format and is not already registered, the password has at least 8 characters including at least one letter and one digit, the two passwords match, and the check box is ticked. The system creates the account with the user’s name, email address and grade, and continues at step 5 of the basic flow. **Alternative flow 2: Incorrect email or password** From step 4 of the basic flow, if no account matches, the system displays the message “The email or password is incorrect.” under the form, keeps the email address and clears the password. The user corrects the information; continue at step 3 of the basic flow. After 5 failed attempts with the same email address within 15 minutes, the system blocks sign-in for that email address for 15 minutes and displays the time at which the user can try again. **Alternative flow 3: Sign-up information is not valid** From step 4 of alternative flow 1, if any check fails, the system highlights each incorrect field and shows what is wrong next to it, for example “This email is already registered. Sign in or use another email.”, “Password must have at least 8 characters, including a letter and a digit.” or “Passwords do not match.” The system keeps the information entered, except the passwords. The user corrects the fields; continue at step 3 of alternative flow 1\. **Alternative flow 4: The user forgot the password** From step 2 of the basic flow, the user clicks ‘Forgot password?’. The system asks for the email address; the user enters it and clicks ‘Send reset link’. The system displays “If this email is registered, we have sent you a password reset link.” and, if the email is registered, sends an email with a reset link that is valid for 30 minutes. The same message is shown whether or not the email is registered, so that nobody can find out which emails have accounts. The user opens the link, enters a new password twice and clicks ‘Reset password’. The system checks the password rules in step 4 of alternative flow 1, saves the new password and displays the Sign in form; continue at step 3 of the basic flow. If the link has expired, the system displays “This link has expired.” and the button ‘Send a new link’; continue at step 2 of this flow. **Alternative flow 5: The user signs out** After step 6 of the basic flow, the user clicks their name and then ‘Sign out’. If all recorded trials have been saved, the system signs the user out, shows the ‘Sign in’ button again and hides ‘History’. If some recorded trials have not been saved yet (for example, because the connection was lost), the system displays “Some results have not been saved yet. Sign out anyway?” with the buttons ‘Cancel’ and ‘Sign out’. ‘Cancel’ keeps the user signed in; ‘Sign out’ continues at step 2 of this flow. **Alternative flow 6: No internet connection** From step 4 of the basic flow or step 4 of alternative flow 1, if the system cannot connect to the PhysiX server, it displays “Cannot connect to PhysiX. Check your internet connection and try again.” with the button ‘Try again’, and keeps the information entered. An experiment in progress continues to work. The user clicks ‘Try again’ when the connection returns; continue at the same step. |
| Pre-conditions | PhysiX is open in a supported browser and the user is not signed in. For signing up, the user has an email address they can access. For resetting a password, the user has a registered account. |
| Post-conditions | If successful, the user is signed in, their name is shown in the top-right corner, and every trial they record from now on is saved to their account. After signing up, a new account exists that stores only the user’s name, email address and grade. After signing out, the user’s saved results remain in their account. If signing in fails, the user remains a guest and can continue to use every feature that does not require an account. |

   2. ## **Use-case:** **UC02 Browse and search experiments**  {#use-case:-uc02-browse-and-search-experiments}

| Use case Name | UC02 \- Browse and search experiments  |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | This use case describes how a user finds an experiment in the Experiment Library, either by grade, chapter and lesson or by searching. The library follows the GDPT 2018 curriculum and the Kết nối tri thức textbooks for Grades 6–12. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | The user opens PhysiX, or clicks ‘Library’ in the top menu. The system displays the Experiment Library with the grade tabs ‘Grade 6’ to ‘Grade 12’ and a search box at the top. The selected grade is the grade the user viewed last, the signed-in user’s grade, or Grade 6 on the first visit. The user clicks a grade tab. The system displays the chapters of that grade in textbook order. Each chapter expands to show its lessons, and each lesson shows its experiment cards. A card shows the experiment name, the lesson number and title, the expected duration and a status label: ‘Available’ or ‘Coming soon’. The user clicks an experiment card with the status ‘Available’.. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. No match: show No experiments found and Clear filters; let the user revise the query. A2. Planned experiment: allow available introduction content to be read, but keep unavailable interactive actions disabled with a reason. A3. Loading fails: keep the query and offer Try again.  |
| Pre-conditions | PhysiX is open in a browser and the computer is connected to the internet. Signing in is not required. |
| Post-conditions | The selected introduction is open, or the library remains usable with the user's search state preserved.  |

   3. ## **Simple Pendulum**

## **Use-case: UC03.1.1 View Simple Pendulum introduction**

| Use case Name | UC03.1.1 \- View Simple Pendulum introduction |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | The user reviews Simple Pendulum before choosing its Guided or unlocked Open Lab workspace. The page structure may be shared; scientific content is experiment-specific. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. From UC02 or History, the user opens this introduction. 2\. The system displays the title, learning objectives, reference lesson if verified, apparatus, principles, assumptions, units and experiment-specific overview. State that l runs from the suspension point to the bob centre; g is fixed at 9.8 m/s²; the release angle is limited to 1°–10°. State the model assumptions: the string is inextensible and massless, the bob is a point mass and air resistance is neglected. The simulation solves the full pendulum equation and is compared with the small-angle reference T \= 2π√(l/g), from which it differs by less than 0.2% within this angle range. Explain the six required trials in three controlled pairs and manual timing of ten complete oscillations per trial.  3\. The system displays Back to library, Start Guided and Open Lab at the bottom-right. Guided is primary. 4\. The system reads the account's successful completion state for this experiment. Open Lab remains visible but disabled until unlocked. 5\. Start Guided opens UC03.1.2. An enabled Open Lab option opens UC03.1.3. Back to library restores the previous library state. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. Open Lab locked: show Complete this Guided experiment once to unlock Open Lab. Do not enter the workspace through a direct link either. A2. Assets or content fail to load: show the failed content and offer retry without claiming the experiment is ready. A3. A signed-in user has already passed this Guided flow: enable its Open Lab option without requiring another pass. A4. Guest unlock handling remains \[NEEDS-VERIFICATION\]; do not invent account-persistence behavior. |
| Pre-conditions | The user selected this experiment. Interactive entry also requires supported graphics and loaded apparatus. |
| Post-conditions | The user stays on the introduction, returns to the library or enters an allowed workspace. Viewing the introduction alone never unlocks Open Lab. |

## **Use-case: UC03.1.2 Perform the Guided Simple Pendulum experiment**

| Use case Name | UC03.1.2 \- Perform the Guided Simple Pendulum experiment |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | The user performs six manually timed trials to investigate length, mass and initial angle. Measurements are shown without correctness feedback during collection. Final success is evaluated through UC03.1.4. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. From UC03.1.1, the user selects Start Guided. The system loads the prepared stand, string, bob, ruler, angle reference and stopwatch, and displays trial 1 of 6 and its required settings. 2\. The user sets the mass and pendulum length using the available apparatus controls and precise inputs. Run stays disabled until both numeric values exactly match the current trial's requirements after unit conversion. 3\. The six required settings are: trial 1: l=0.50 m, m=100 g, angle=10°; trial 2: 1.00 m, 100 g, 10°; trial 3: 0.80 m, 50 g, 10°; trial 4: 0.80 m, 200 g, 10°; trial 5: 0.80 m, 100 g, 10°; trial 6: 0.80 m, 100 g, 5°. Within each pair only the named variable changes. 4\. The user clicks Run. The stopwatch remains at zero. Only the bob can now be dragged to prepare release; other experiment controls are locked. 5\. The user holds and drags the bob to the requested initial angle and releases it. Motion starts, but the stopwatch does not. From release until Stop timer, all in-experiment interaction is blocked except the stopwatch button and T key. A visual reference may mark the released string and bob position. \[NEEDS-VERIFICATION: angle tolerance and release validation.\] 6\. The user clicks Start timer or presses T. The stopwatch starts at that actual input time. It is not backdated or aligned automatically to the release. 7\. The user observes and counts ten complete oscillations, then clicks Stop timer or presses T again. The system freezes both the stopwatch and pendulum at that instant; it never auto-stops at ten oscillations. 8\. The system fills a provisional row with the actual settings, required N=10, measured elapsed time t and Tmeasured=t/10. It does not show theoretical answers, error percentages or Pass/Fail during collection. 9\. For trials 1-5, Next trial confirms this provisional measurement and advances the progress count. The system returns the apparatus and stopwatch to preparation for the next required settings, which the user adjusts. Retry instead discards only the unconfirmed measurement and repeats the same trial. 10\. At trial 6, Confirm measurements confirms the final row and opens UC03.1.4. An automatically filled row does not itself count as confirmed. 11\. In UC03.1.4 the user compares the three pairs, selects correct statements/formulas in four conclusion groups and submits the conclusions. Only a passing result completes Guided and unlocks this account's Pendulum Open Lab. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. Wrong preparation: keep Run disabled; identify the incorrect length or mass and the exact required value. Equivalent values such as 0.5 m and 0.50 m are equal. A2. Out-of-order input: ignore timer input before release; after release allow Start only; while timing allow Stop only; after Stop do not restart that measurement with T. Holding T must not generate repeated start/stop actions. A3. Interaction while moving: do not change parameters, drag the bob again, move the camera, open AI or switch steps between release and Stop. Preserve the scene and show the relevant timing instruction. A4. Retry before final assessment: clear only the provisional reading, reset the stopwatch to zero and return the current trial to preparation. Keep all confirmed trials. A5. Invalid numerical sample or rendering/calculation failure: stop the current measurement, explain the problem and allow recovery of that measurement while keeping confirmed trials. Do not fabricate a sample. A6. Failed final assessment: the attempt fails. Offer Restart experiment or Exit experiment only; do not permit repairing a single trial/pair or resubmitting conclusions within the failed attempt. Restart requires confirmation before clearing the attempt's working data and starts again at trial 1\. Previously saved sessions are not deleted. A7. Leave before completion: do not record a successful completion or new unlock. Warn before abandoning recorded working data. Browser/OS controls are outside the in-experiment interaction lock. A8. AI guidance: UC05 may be used when the workspace is not in its release-to-Stop locked interval. AI cannot run the stopwatch, confirm data or bypass validation on the user's behalf. A9. Release angle outside the allowed range: the bob cannot be dragged beyond 10°, and a release below 1° is not accepted. The system keeps the bob at the last valid position and shows “Allowed release angle: 1°–10°”.  |
| Pre-conditions | UC03.1.1 is open; the prepared apparatus is loaded. l means suspension point to bob centre. g=9.8 m/s² is fixed. Successful account-based completion requires an identifiable account; guest association remains pending. |
| Post-conditions | Success: six trials are confirmed, all six individual errors and all three pair errors are at most 5%, and all four conclusion groups are correct. Mark Guided completed and unlock only Pendulum Open Lab for that account. Failure/exit: do not create a successful completion; preserve any unlock earned by an earlier successful attempt. |

## **Use-case: UC03.1.3 Perform the Simple Pendulum experiment in Open Lab**

| Use case Name | UC03.1.3 \- Perform the Simple Pendulum experiment in Open Lab |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | The unlocked user uses the same Pendulum apparatus and manual-timing tools without a Guided step list, required six-trial plan or Guided conclusion assessment. This is a proposed elaboration of the owner's Open Lab definition. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Enter through the enabled bottom-right Open Lab option on its introduction or successful Guided result. Check availability and this account's Pendulum unlock. 2\. Load stand, string, bob, ruler, angle reference and stopwatch. Show live length, mass, angle and stopwatch readings; keep g=9.8 m/s² and l measured from pivot to bob centre. 3\. Between runs, change supported l/m values through apparatus manipulation or precise inputs and choose the intended positive integer oscillation count N. The release angle is limited to 1°–10°, the domain in which the small-angle reference applies. 4\. Run arms release without starting the clock. Drag and release the bob; press T or Start timer to begin the actual measured interval and T or Stop timer to freeze motion and clock. Do not impose automatic theoretical timing. 5\. Review the provisional row, then Retry or Record trial. Repeat with self-chosen supported conditions; preserve confirmed rows across a trial reset. 6\. Open UC03.1.4 to graph/compare the labeled Open Lab records or UC08 to export. Exit/Restart experiment use the shared preservation/confirmation rules. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. No corresponding account unlock or unavailable workspace: refuse entry and explain the applicable reason. A2. Invalid l/m, N≤0/noninteger or a missing timing interval: block the affected run/record and show the required correction. A3. Parameter changes while a timed run is active require stopping/resetting that unconfirmed run first; retained recordings are immutable. A4. Release angle outside 1°–10°: the system does not accept the release and shows the allowed range. Larger amplitudes are future scope; when added, their data must be labelled as outside the small-angle reference domain. Never claim angle independence for arbitrary amplitudes. A5. No Guided pass/fail or six-trial checklist is applied. Open Lab records cannot grant Guided completion. |
| Pre-conditions | This account has a successful Pendulum Guided completion and the workspace is available. Open Lab uses the Guided parameter bounds (l 0.20–1.50 m, m 50–200 g, release angle 1°–10°); the length and mass bounds remain subject to review by the physics content expert. |
| Post-conditions | Valid self-selected Pendulum snapshots are available for Results/export. The prior unlock remains unchanged; history auto-save policy remains pending. |

## **Use-case: UC03.1.4 View and compare Simple Pendulum results**

| Use case Name | UC03.1.4 \- View and compare Simple Pendulum results |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | This use case records and presents Pendulum trial snapshots, plots and comparisons for both modes. Guided additionally requires six measurements, three pair checks and a four-part conclusion. Open Lab does not inherit Guided completion requirements. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. While collecting data, each stopped measurement produces a provisional row. Next trial or Confirm measurements commits it; the confirmed settings and readings then remain snapshots. 2\. The user opens Results. The table identifies trial, mode, l (m), m (g), release angle (°), required N, measured t (s), Tmeasured=t/N (s), and, at the final Guided result, Treference=2π√(l/9.8) (s) and individual deviation (%). N=10 is the requested count, not proof that the user counted correctly. 3\. The system plots the measured T against l and the reference curve with units and a legend. For the three Guided pairs, show the settings held constant and the measured period comparison. Do not average periods at different lengths into one experimental period. 4\. The user selects compatible trials for comparison. The system keeps mode labels and experimental conditions explicit. For Open Lab, present available records without applying the six-trial Guided checklist. 5\. For final Guided assessment, calculate each individual deviation as \|Tmeasured-Treference\|/Treference × 100%. All six must be ≤5%; compare unrounded values. 6\. For each Guided pair, set Rmeasured=Tlater/Tearlier. Rrequired is √(l2/l1)=√2 for trials 1-2, and 1 for trials 3-4 and 5-6. Pair deviation is \|Rmeasured-Rrequired\|/Rrequired × 100%. All three must be ≤5%. Passing individual checks does not imply passing pair checks. 7\. The user ticks correct statements/formulas in four groups: Effect of string length, Effect of bob mass, Effect of initial angle, and Period formula. The correct scientific content is T proportional to √l; no mass dependence in the ideal model; near independence from angle within the small-angle domain; and T=2π√(l/g). Do not preselect answers. \[NEEDS-VERIFICATION: candidate statements and exact answer sets.\] 8\. The user selects Submit conclusions. The system assesses the four groups and the measurement checks. A pass requires all four groups correct, all six individual checks passing and all three pair checks passing. 9\. On pass, mark Guided completed for this account and enable Pendulum Open Lab. Offer Open Lab, Export report (UC08) and return navigation. On failure, show Not passed with reasons and offer Restart experiment or Exit experiment only. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. No recorded data: show an empty state. Do not fabricate rows, curves or a successful completion. A2. Incompatible comparison: explain why selected quantities/conditions cannot be compared directly; retain the records. A3. Incomplete final data or missing conclusions: do not produce a pass. The UI explains missing inputs; final scoring is not performed during trial collection. A4. A measurement, pair relationship or conclusion fails: do not unlock on this attempt. The complete attempt must be restarted or exited; no selective trial correction after failure. A5. Graph rendering fails: keep the table and records, offer retry and avoid drawing a substitute theoretical dataset as measured results. A6. Saved history result: display recorded values read-only. Starting a new experiment does not overwrite the saved entry. A7. Open Lab result: omit Guided completion questions and unlocking effects. Preserve mode labels when displaying or exporting. \[NEEDS-VERIFICATION: whether Open Lab sessions persist in History.\] |
| Pre-conditions | A current Pendulum workspace or an accessible saved Pendulum result is open. Full Guided assessment requires six confirmed trial rows. |
| Post-conditions | Results are displayed without changing recorded values. Only a passing Guided result grants completion/unlock. Open Lab viewing or export never grants Guided completion. |

4. ## **Ohm's Law**

## **Use-case: UC03.2.1 View Ohm's Law introduction**

| Use case Name | UC03.2.1 \- View Ohm's Law introduction |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Review Grade 11 lesson 23 objectives, conductor X/Y setup, assumptions and available modes before entering the dedicated electrical workspace. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Open the Ohm introduction from UC02 or an Ohm history item. 2\. Read objectives: obtain U-I pairs for two identified conductors, inspect U/I where defined, plot I against U and compare series under fixed conductor conditions. 3\. Review adjustable DC source, switch, conductors X/Y, series ammeter and parallel voltmeter; I=U/R applies to an ohmic conductor at unchanged temperature/conditions. 4\. Review direct terminal wiring, source-voltage precision control, live U (V)/I (mA), conductor labels and connection validation. Retained baseline bounds of 0–12 V are a proposed plan, subject to component ratings. 5\. Choose Back to library or primary Start Guided (UC03.2.2). The bottom-right Open Lab option (UC03.2.3) is visible and disabled with the completion reason until this account has passed Ohm Guided once; check availability separately. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. Locked direct Open Lab link: refuse entry and explain Complete this Guided experiment once to unlock Open Lab. A2. Unsupported graphics or missing assets: keep content readable and offer retry without entering a broken circuit. A3. The workbook NTC extension is not a separate catalogue row and is not silently included in the linear-law proof. Show it only as future scope with its distinct nonlinear model. |
| Pre-conditions | The user selected Ohm; introduction content exists. Account/guest association remains a shared pending item. |
| Post-conditions | The selected allowed workspace is opened, or the user returns to Library. Introduction viewing never grants an unlock. |

## **Use-case: UC03.2.2 Perform the Guided Ohm's Law experiment**

| Use case Name | UC03.2.2 \- Perform the Guided Ohm's Law experiment |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Draft electrical procedure based on VL11-B23 row 11: measure conductor X then Y over distinct source settings and determine whether their individual U-I relationships support the Ohm model. Bao must cross-check physics/units; this is not a new approval claim. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Load source, switch, X/Y conductors, meters and experiment-specific circuit diagram. Show wiring preparation as the first Guided step. 2\. With the switch open and source at zero, select conductor X, wire the ammeter in series and voltmeter across X using the 3D terminals. Precision controls expose source voltage and meter ranges. 3\. Validate the circuit, selected meter mode/range, polarity and allowed source/component bounds. Only then enable switch closure. 4\. Close the switch, vary source voltage to the next assigned value and wait for valid live U and I. Proposed plan: at least five distinct nonzero voltage points per conductor within 0–12 V and component ratings; exact targets are reviewable configuration. 5\. Record each settled U/I pair as a snapshot. Retry affects an unconfirmed reading; Next setting advances only after Record trial. Do not replace the reading with a formula answer. 6\. Open the switch and return the source to zero. Replace X with Y, revalidate connections, and repeat the five-point series with conductor identity retained. 7\. Open the switch and confirm both series. Enter UC03.2.4, compare I-U slopes/U/I and submit the conductor-model conclusions. 8\. Proposed completion: both valid five-point series and correct conclusions on proportionality, resistance and model limits. If a selected conductor is non-ohmic, identifying its nonlinear behavior is valid analysis; never force a false linear conclusion. Exact conductor models/answer key must be configured before assessment can grant a new unlock. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. Open/short circuit, meter in wrong position, reversed/range-invalid measurement: identify the fault and block unsafe/undefined operation, with recoverable wiring feedback. A2. Change conductor/wires only with switch open and source safe. Confirmed X/Y points are retained as separate series. A3. At U=I=0, a point may be retained as a labeled origin but U/I is undefined and does not count toward the five nonzero settings. A4. Duplicate voltage does not advance distinct-setting progress; preserve it as a repeat only if the user deliberately records one. A5. Missing required series/conclusions: explain missing work without inventing a pass. Incorrect conclusions may be reviewed in this draft; the Pendulum restart-only failure rule is not copied. A6. AI may explain wiring or graph interpretation, but applies changes only after confirmation and circuit revalidation. |
| Pre-conditions | UC03.2.1 opened an available workspace. Conductor models, source ratings, plan targets and conclusion key are configured for this draft flow. |
| Post-conditions | Confirmed X/Y snapshots are available. Successful final assessment marks Ohm Guided completed and unlocks only Ohm Open Lab for that account; incomplete/invalid/unconfigured assessment cannot grant it. |

## **Use-case: UC03.2.4 View and compare Ohm's Law results**

| Use case Name | UC03.2.4 \- View and compare Ohm's Law results |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Present X/Y electrical results from Guided or Open Lab, including the actual recorded table, experiment-specific graphs and conductor comparison. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Load confirmed snapshots from the current workspace or an accessible saved result; keep session, mode and conductor identity. 2\. Show trial, conductor ID, mode, U (V), I (mA), SI current (A), meter metadata and U/I (Ω) where defined. 3\. Plot I (A or mA, explicitly labeled) against U (V) with separate X/Y series. An ohmic series has I-U slope 1/R when I is in A; convert mA slopes before calculating Ω. 4\. Compare each conductor's U/I consistency, fitted trend and conditions. Never infer an ohmic constant from a nonlinear series without explaining its model. 5\. Guided: show required-series progress, then ask whether each series supports proportionality and what its resistance/slope means. Assess the configured answer key and valid data before marking completion/unlocking. 6\. Open Lab: show only available electrical data, analysis and comparisons, without Guided progress/questions. Offer UC08 using the same raw snapshots and experiment template. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. No data: display an empty state; insufficient distinct points: retain rows but disable a misleading fit/assessment. A2. At I=0, display a dash for U/I and exclude it from a ratio average; do not turn it into 0 Ω. A3. Different conductor temperatures/models or incompatible meter ranges: identify the conditions and keep comparisons explicitly separated. A4. Graph/AI/export failure does not erase the electrical table or change completion. Saved results stay read-only. |
| Pre-conditions | An Ohm result is selected. Guided assessment additionally requires both configured series and conclusion inputs. |
| Post-conditions | Labeled electrical results are displayed. A configured successful Guided assessment grants its account unlock; viewing/exporting or Open Lab analysis does not. |

5. ## **Measurement Uncertainty**

## **Use-case: UC03.3.1 View Measurement Uncertainty introduction**

| Use case Name | UC03.3.1 \- View Measurement Uncertainty introduction |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Measure a toy car travel distance and time repeatedly; distinguish direct and indirect measurements and report average speed with uncertainty. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Open this experiment introduction from Library or an accessible matching History result. 2\. Read its own objective, lesson/source status, apparatus and assumptions. Objective: Measure a toy car travel distance and time repeatedly; distinguish direct and indirect measurements and report average speed with uncertainty. 3\. Review apparatus: Battery-powered toy car, marked track, ruler with 1 mm divisions and stopwatch with 0.01 s resolution. 4\. Review model and limits: Average speed is distance divided by travel time, not the final instantaneous speed. Use the workbook convention: mean absolute deviation plus half an instrument division for total absolute uncertainty; this is a stated teaching convention, not a universal instrument rule. 5\. Review available manipulation/precision controls: Drag markers A/B and align the ruler zero; use precise marker positions in m and stopwatch Start/Stop. Instrument resolution is visible and read-only in Guided. 6\. Show Back to library, primary Start Guided (UC03.3.2) and bottom-right Open Lab (UC03.3.3). Check implementation availability and this account's successful completion of this same Guided experiment; locked Open Lab stays visible with its reason. 7\. Navigate only to an available, authorized mode; otherwise keep the readable introduction with the appropriate disabled-state explanation. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. No prior success for this experiment: disable Open Lab and show Complete this Guided experiment once to unlock Open Lab; enforce the same rule for direct entry. A2. Planned/unavailable workspace: disable interactive start actions with Planned experiment even if the introduction exists. A3. Missing content/assets: identify the failed section and offer retry without claiming apparatus readiness. |
| Pre-conditions | The selected catalogue record and readable introduction exist. No account is needed merely to read this page. |
| Post-conditions | The user remains on the introduction, returns to the previous library state or enters an available authorized workspace. Reading does not grant completion/unlock. |

## **Use-case: UC03.3.2 Perform the Guided Measurement Uncertainty experiment**

| Use case Name | UC03.3.2 \- Perform the Guided Measurement Uncertainty experiment |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Perform the experiment using its apparatus-specific Guided procedure, direct manipulation and precision controls. Measure a toy car travel distance and time repeatedly; distinguish direct and indirect measurements and report average speed with uncertainty. This generated procedure is a draft for scientific/UX review. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Select Start Guided. Load the dedicated apparatus, display the current step and required data plan. Plan: Five confirmed distance-time trials for the same A/B interval and start condition, as listed in the workbook. 2\. Place A at the car release position and B ahead of A. Align the ruler zero and read the distance s; the car starts from rest at A. 3\. Verify positive separation and the assigned ruler/stopwatch resolution. Reset the car and stopwatch without clearing confirmed trials. 4\. Release the car and operate the stopwatch for its passage from A to B. Preserve the actual user timing interval; do not substitute an ideal travel time. 5\. Stop the timing, inspect s and t, then choose Retry or Record trial. Record commits a snapshot; an unconfirmed reading does not advance progress. 6\. Repeat until five trials have been confirmed with the same interval and apparatus. A changed interval starts a separate series rather than contaminating the mean. 7\. Open Results, calculate the means and absolute/relative uncertainties, and submit the experiment-specific interpretation. 8\. At each measurement stage, validate the current preparation/measurement before enabling the next action; show live readings with units. Record trial commits an immutable row/run, and the next required stage unlocks only after confirmation. 9\. At UC03.3.4, require the complete configured plan, valid recorded data and correct experiment-specific conclusions: Direct measurements are s and t; v is derived. Repetition estimates scatter but does not remove every systematic error. A quoted result requires value, unit and uncertainty. 10\. Only successful assessment marks this Guided experiment completed and unlocks its own Open Lab for the account. Show completion and return/Open Lab/export options; do not unlock a different experiment. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. B is at/before A or outside the track: retain the last valid markers and disable release until corrected. A2. Stopwatch not started, t≤0, or the car has not completed the interval: do not record an average speed; offer Retry. A3. Instrument resolution or measured units are missing: block the affected uncertainty calculation and identify the missing metadata. A4. Out-of-order or disabled action: retain the last valid apparatus/data state, explain the prerequisite and enable a recoverable correction; do not advance by clicking Next alone. A5. Retry clears only the current unconfirmed measurement/run. Restart experiment asks confirmation before clearing the full working attempt; retain previously saved history. A6. Missing plan values, apparatus calibration or answer key: show Assessment unavailable and do not grant a completion/unlock. AI failure leaves built-in instructions usable. |
| Pre-conditions | The workspace is available, introduction UC03.3.1 is selected, and the versioned plan/ranges/models/answer key are configured. These draft numeric choices remain subject to review. |
| Post-conditions | Valid confirmed measurements and observations are available in UC03.3.4. A successful configured assessment grants only this account's experiment-specific completion/unlock; incomplete or failed work does not. Previously earned unlocks are retained. |

## **Use-case: UC03.3.4 View and compare Measurement Uncertainty results**

| Use case Name | UC03.3.4 \- View and compare Measurement Uncertainty results |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Show the actual experiment-specific results from Guided or Open Lab, including recorded tables, relevant graphs and compatible multi-trial/condition comparisons. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Load confirmed snapshots from the current workspace or accessible saved result. Keep session, experiment, branch, configuration and mode labels; provisional readings remain distinct. 2\. Present these data fields with units: Trial; mode; interval/series ID; s (m); t (s); instrument resolutions; v=s/t (m/s). 3\. Derive analysis from those same raw records: For A=s or t: mean(A), mean absolute deviation, ΔA=mean absolute deviation+half the displayed resolution, and δA=100ΔA/mean(A). Calculate v=mean(s)/mean(t) and δv≈δs+δt for the quotient; report v with Δv=vδv/100 and consistent significant figures. 4\. Show graphs: Trial versus s and trial versus t with means/uncertainty; average-speed comparison with uncertainty bars. These are repeated measurements, not a trajectory reconstruction. 5\. Allow compatible selection/comparison: Compare repeats only within a common interval, start condition and instrument configuration; compare separate series with their conditions and uncertainties shown. 6\. For Guided, show the configured plan progress and collect the specific conclusion response(s): Direct measurements are s and t; v is derived. Repetition estimates scatter but does not remove every systematic error. A quoted result requires value, unit and uncertainty. All required data and all configured conclusion responses must be valid/correct before successful completion. 7\. For Open Lab, show its available data/analysis without the Guided checklist or completion effect. Offer Export report (UC08) from the same source snapshots; templates include this experiment's quantities and assumptions. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. No confirmed data: show an empty state without invented rows or a completion badge. A2. Missing denominator, calibration, model condition or insufficient fit points: keep raw data and show the derived value/fit as unavailable with a reason. A3. Incompatible units, branches or conditions: explain the comparison restriction and keep the selected records intact. A4. Graph rendering fails: retain the labeled table and retry; never substitute theoretical values as measured samples. A5. Incomplete plan/conclusions cannot pass. Proposed new-experiment conclusions may be reviewed/corrected; raw confirmed measurements remain immutable. The Pendulum restart-only failure policy is not implied. A6. Saved history is read-only. Export/AI errors do not change the observations, completion or unlock. |
| Pre-conditions | A matching current or saved result is selected. Numerical analysis requires its declared physical inputs; Guided assessment additionally requires the configured plan and answer key. |
| Post-conditions | The experiment-specific result, graph/comparison or explicit unavailable state is shown without rewriting records. Only successful configured Guided assessment grants the corresponding account unlock. |

## **Use-case: UC03.4.1 View Average and Instantaneous Speed introduction**

| Use case Name | UC03.4.1 \- View Average and Instantaneous Speed introduction |

| Brief description | Measure average speed between two photogates and approximate instantaneous speed at a photogate using the ball diameter. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Open this experiment introduction from Library or an accessible matching History result. 2\. Read its own objective, lesson/source status, apparatus and assumptions. Objective: Measure average speed between two photogates and approximate instantaneous speed at a photogate using the ball diameter. 3\. Review apparatus: Inclined track, steel ball, electromagnetic release, two photogates E/F, digital timer, plumb line, angle scale and vernier calipers. 4\. Review model and limits: For two-gate timing v\_average=s\_EF/t\_EF. For one-gate interruption v\_instantaneous≈d/t\_gate, provided speed changes little during beam interruption. The two quantities describe different intervals. 5\. Review available manipulation/precision controls: Drag the gates along the track and connect E/F to A/B; select MODE A↔B or A/B. Drag caliper jaws around the ball and read/enter d precisely; adjust track angle within supported bounds. 6\. Show Back to library, primary Start Guided (UC03.4.2) and bottom-right Open Lab (UC03.4.3). Check implementation availability and this account's successful completion of this same Guided experiment; locked Open Lab stays visible with its reason. 7\. Navigate only to an available, authorized mode; otherwise keep the readable introduction with the appropriate disabled-state explanation. |

Planned/unavailable workspace: disable interactive start actions with Planned experiment even if the introduction exists. A3. Missing content/assets: identify the failed section and offer retry without claiming apparatus readiness. |

| Use case Name | UC03.4.2 \- Perform the Guided Average and Instantaneous Speed experiment |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Perform the experiment using its apparatus-specific Guided procedure, direct manipulation and precision controls. Measure average speed between two photogates and approximate instantaneous speed at a photogate using the ball diameter. This generated procedure is a draft for scientific/UX review. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Select Start Guided. Load the dedicated apparatus, display the current step and required data plan. Plan: Two branches in this workbook entry: three confirmed two-gate trials and three confirmed single-gate trials. 2\. Mount and align the inclined track. Position E before F, connect E/F to the correct timer sockets and measure their separation s\_EF. 3\. Select MODE A↔B for the average-speed branch. Place the ball against the electromagnet and press Reset timer. 4\. Release the ball. The photogate events start and stop the digital timer automatically; read t\_EF without introducing a manual-stopwatch interval. 5\. Record or retry the reading. Complete three releases using the same track and gate positions for this branch. 6\. Open the instantaneous-speed branch. Measure the ball diameter d with the calipers and choose the single-gate timer MODE A or B matching the selected gate. 7\. Reset and release the ball; record its beam-interruption duration. Complete three releases at the same gate position. 8\. Confirm both branch tables, then inspect speed estimates and uncertainties in Results and submit the comparison. 9\. At each measurement stage, validate the current preparation/measurement before enabling the next action; show live readings with units. Record trial commits an immutable row/run, and the next required stage unlocks only after confirmation. 10\. At UC03.4.4, require the complete configured plan, valid recorded data and correct experiment-specific conclusions: Average speed uses displacement along the measured interval and its elapsed time. A short beam-interruption interval approximates local speed. Timer mode determines what interval was measured. 11\. Only successful assessment marks this Guided experiment completed and unlocks its own Open Lab for the account. Show completion and return/Open Lab/export options; do not unlock a different experiment. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. Wrong timer mode/socket, reversed two-gate order or an unaligned beam: disable release/recording and highlight the connection to correct. A2. d≤0, gate separation≤0 or t≤0: keep the reading provisional and show the invalid quantity. A3. A gate or track angle changes mid-run: stop that unconfirmed run and request a fresh release; retain confirmed series. A4. Out-of-order or disabled action: retain the last valid apparatus/data state, explain the prerequisite and enable a recoverable correction; do not advance by clicking Next alone. A5. Retry clears only the current unconfirmed measurement/run. Restart experiment asks confirmation before clearing the full working attempt; retain previously saved history. A6. Missing plan values, apparatus calibration or answer key: show Assessment unavailable and do not grant a completion/unlock. AI failure leaves built-in instructions usable. |

Review apparatus: Vertical track and plumb line, steel cylinder, release mechanism, photogate and digital timer. 4\. Review model and limits: With release from rest, negligible drag and a timer measuring release-to-reference passage, s=gt²/2 and g=2s/t². The photogate reference and cylinder geometry must match the measured height. 5\. Review available manipulation/precision controls: Drag the photogate vertically, align the plumb line, read/set the release-to-trigger distance in m, and operate Reset and Release. 6\. Show Back to library, primary Start Guided (UC03.5.2) and bottom-right Open Lab (UC03.5.3). ## **Use-case: UC03.5.2 Perform the Guided Free Fall Acceleration experiment**

| Use case Name | UC03.5.2 \- Perform the Guided Free Fall Acceleration experiment |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Perform the experiment using its apparatus-specific Guided procedure, direct manipulation and precision controls. Estimate g from a body released from rest at measured drop heights and inspect the distance-time-squared relationship. This generated procedure is a draft for scientific/UX review. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Select Start Guided. Load the dedicated apparatus, display the current step and required data plan. Plan: Five confirmed releases at each required height. Draft plan: two distinct positive heights in the configured track range; the workbook does not specify their numeric values or number of heights. 2\. Align the track vertically with the plumb reference. Place the cylinder at rest in the release mechanism. 3\. Position the photogate at the first assigned height and read s from the declared release/trigger references. Validate alignment and positive clearance. 4\. Select the release-to-gate timer mode and press Reset timer. Release the cylinder without an initial push. 5\. Read the automatic travel time, then Retry or Record trial. Repeat until five releases at this height are confirmed. 6\. Move the gate to the second configured height and perform the same five-release series. Record the actual height for every row. 7\. Open Results, compare g estimates and the s-versus-t² fit, and submit the model/measurement conclusion. 8\. At each measurement stage, validate the current preparation/measurement before enabling the next action; show live readings with units. Record trial commits an immutable row/run, and the next required stage unlocks only after confirmation. 9\. At UC03.5.4, require the complete configured plan, valid recorded data and correct experiment-specific conclusions: For a release from rest, distance is proportional to t². Doubling drop height does not double fall time. A timing/height offset can bias the inferred g. 10\. Only successful assessment marks this Guided experiment completed and unlocks its own Open Lab for the account. Show completion and return/Open Lab/export options; do not unlock a different experiment. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. Cylinder path misses the beam or track is not vertical: keep Release disabled and show alignment feedback. A2. Release point, gate height or timer mode changes while falling: invalidate only the current unconfirmed run and reset safely. A3. Zero/negative s or t, or a gate-block duration used instead of flight time: do not calculate g; explain the required timing interval. A4. Out-of-order or disabled action: retain the last valid apparatus/data state, explain the prerequisite and enable a recoverable correction; do not advance by clicking Next alone. A5. Retry clears only the current unconfirmed measurement/run. Restart experiment asks confirmation before clearing the full working attempt; retain previously saved history. A6. 8. ## **Resultant of Forces**

## **Use-case: UC03.6.1 View Resultant of Forces introduction**

| Use case Name | UC03.6.1 \- View Resultant of Forces introduction |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Determine the resultant of two concurrent forces and of two parallel forces acting in the same direction. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Open this experiment introduction from Library or an accessible matching History result. 2\. Read its own objective, lesson/source status, apparatus and assumptions. Objective: Determine the resultant of two concurrent forces and of two parallel forces acting in the same direction. 3\. Review apparatus: Steel board, magnets, rubber band and strings, spring balances, protractor and marker; parallel-force branch additionally uses a bar, weights and a sliding hook. 4\. Review model and limits: Concurrent: F²=F1²+F2²+2F1F2 cos α. Parallel, same direction: F=F1+F2 and F1d1=F2d2, where distances are from the resultant line of action. 5\. Review available manipulation/precision controls: Drag anchors and force directions on the board, rotate the protractor, adjust tensions/weights, drag the replacement-force hook and read exact N, ° and m controls. 6\. Show Back to library, primary Start Guided (UC03.6.2) and bottom-right Open Lab (UC03.6.3). Check implementation availability and this account's successful completion of this same Guided experiment; locked Open Lab stays visible with its reason. 7\. Navigate only to an available, authorized mode; otherwise keep the readable introduction with the appropriate disabled-state explanation. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. No prior success for this experiment: disable Open Lab and show Complete this Guided experiment once to unlock Open Lab; enforce the same rule for direct entry. A2. Planned/unavailable workspace: disable interactive start actions with Planned experiment even if the introduction exists. A3. Missing content/assets: identify the failed section and offer retry without claiming apparatus readiness. |
| Pre-conditions | The selected catalogue record and readable introduction exist. No account is needed merely to read this page. |
| Post-conditions | The user remains on the introduction, returns to the previous library state or enters an available authorized workspace. Reading does not grant completion/unlock. |

## **Use-case: UC03.6.2 Perform the Guided Resultant of Forces experiment**

| Use case Name | UC03.6.2 \- Perform the Guided Resultant of Forces experiment |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Perform the experiment using its apparatus-specific Guided procedure, direct manipulation and precision controls. Determine the resultant of two concurrent forces and of two parallel forces acting in the same direction. This generated procedure is a draft for scientific/UX review. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Select Start Guided. Load the dedicated apparatus, display the current step and required data plan. Plan: Concurrent branch: at least three confirmed matched-position measurements. Parallel branch: draft minimum three different weight/position configurations; its count is not stated in the workbook. 2\. Select Concurrent forces, align strings in the board plane and centre the protractor at their meeting point. 3\. Apply F1 and F2, adjust the angle, and mark the equilibrium reference point A. Record both balance readings and α. 4\. Replace the two pulls with one spring balance. Adjust its direction and magnitude until the same point A is restored, then record the measured resultant. 5\. Repeat for at least three assigned force/angle settings; keep each pair and its matched replacement reading together. 6\. Select Parallel forces. Hang the assigned weights at the bar ends and mark the initial level position. 7\. Move the combined weights to the sliding hook and adjust its position until the bar returns to the marked level position. 8\. Record F1, F2, resultant F and distances d1/d2 for the configured parallel-force series. 9\. Open the two separate result sections and conclude how vector addition and moment balance determine the resultant. 10\. At each measurement stage, validate the current preparation/measurement before enabling the next action; show live readings with units. Record trial commits an immutable row/run, and the next required stage unlocks only after confirmation. 11\. At UC03.6.4, require the complete configured plan, valid recorded data and correct experiment-specific conclusions: Concurrent force addition depends on the included angle. Same-direction parallel forces add in magnitude, while their line of action follows moment balance. 12\. Only successful assessment marks this Guided experiment completed and unlocks its own Open Lab for the account. Show completion and return/Open Lab/export options; do not unlock a different experiment. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. Strings leave the board plane or the protractor is off-centre: prevent confirmation and show the misalignment. A2. Replacement force does not restore the marked point/bar position: keep the measurement provisional and allow adjustment. A3. Parallel branch uses opposing force directions or an undefined distance ratio: show that the selected procedure is for same-direction forces; do not apply its formula silently. A4. Out-of-order or disabled action: retain the last valid apparatus/data state, explain the prerequisite and enable a recoverable correction; do not advance by clicking Next alone. A5. Retry clears only the current unconfirmed measurement/run. Restart experiment asks confirmation before clearing the full working attempt; retain previously saved history. A6. Missing plan values, apparatus calibration or answer key: show Assessment unavailable and do not grant a completion/unlock. AI failure leaves built-in instructions usable. |

## **Use-case: UC03.7.1 View Momentum in Collisions introduction**

| Use case Name | UC03.7.1 \- View Momentum in Collisions introduction |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Measure signed cart velocities before and after sticking and separating collisions and examine momentum conservation for an approximately isolated system. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Open this experiment introduction from Library or an accessible matching History result. 2\. Read its own objective, lesson/source status, apparatus and assumptions. Objective: Measure signed cart velocities before and after sticking and separating collisions and examine momentum conservation for an approximately isolated system. 3\. Review apparatus: Level air track and air supply, two carts with flags and collision attachments, two photogates, digital timer and balance. 4\. Review model and limits: v=s\_flag/t with sign from travel direction; p=mv. Compare total signed momentum before and after. Near-isolation does not imply kinetic energy is conserved in every collision. 5\. Review available manipulation/precision controls: Drag carts and photogates, adjust track level and air supply, weigh each complete cart, set flag lengths precisely, and select sticking or separating attachments. 6\. Show Back to library, primary Start Guided (UC03.7.2) and bottom-right Open Lab (UC03.7.3). Check implementation availability and this account's successful completion of this same Guided experiment; locked Open Lab stays visible with its reason. 7\. Navigate only to an available, authorized mode; otherwise keep the readable introduction with the appropriate disabled-state explanation. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. No prior success for this experiment: disable Open Lab and show Complete this Guided experiment once to unlock Open Lab; enforce the same rule for direct entry. A2. Planned/unavailable workspace: disable interactive start actions with Planned experiment even if the introduction exists. A3. Missing content/assets: identify the failed section and offer retry without claiming apparatus readiness. |
| Pre-conditions | The selected catalogue record and readable introduction exist. No account is needed merely to read this page. |
| Post-conditions | The user remains on the introduction, returns to the previous library state or enters an available authorized workspace. Reading does not grant completion/unlock. |

## **Use-case: UC03.7.2 Perform the Guided Momentum in Collisions experiment**

| Use case Name | UC03.7.2 \- Perform the Guided Momentum in Collisions experiment |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Perform the experiment using its apparatus-specific Guided procedure, direct manipulation and precision controls. Measure signed cart velocities before and after sticking and separating collisions and examine momentum conservation for an approximately isolated system. This generated procedure is a draft for scientific/UX review. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Select Start Guided. Load the dedicated apparatus, display the current step and required data plan. Plan: Both collision branches required. Draft minimum three confirmed valid collisions per branch, with at least one direction-sensitive comparison; repeat count is proposed, not specified in the workbook. 2\. Level the track, enable the air supply, weigh both assembled carts and measure each interruption flag length. 3\. Place the gates so their before/after passages can be assigned to the correct cart. Declare the positive track direction and verify the event labels. 4\. Choose the separating-collision attachments, position carts and reset the timer. Apply the assigned launch condition. 5\. Run the collision and capture the valid before/after gate events. Inspect cart identity and direction before confirming the snapshot. 6\. Repeat the required separating-collision trials, keeping masses and flags associated with their cart IDs. 7\. Fit the sticking attachments and reweigh if the assembled masses change. Run and record the sticking-collision series, including the common final velocity. 8\. Open Results and compare total signed momenta and, separately, kinetic energies. Submit the isolation/conservation conclusions. 9\. At each measurement stage, validate the current preparation/measurement before enabling the next action; show live readings with units. Record trial commits an immutable row/run, and the next required stage unlocks only after confirmation. 10\. At UC03.7.4, require the complete configured plan, valid recorded data and correct experiment-specific conclusions: Momentum is a signed/vector quantity. Approximate conservation requires small external impulse. Sticking collisions need not conserve kinetic energy. 11\. Only successful assessment marks this Guided experiment completed and unlocks its own Open Lab for the account. Show completion and return/Open Lab/export options; do not unlock a different experiment. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. Air supply off or track not level: block the assigned measurement and prompt apparatus preparation. A2. Overlapping/missing gate events or unidentified cart/direction: do not infer an arbitrary velocity; allow a fresh trial. A3. Zero flag duration, collision outside the observed region or a mass changed without reweighing: reject the provisional record and identify the cause. A4. Out-of-order or disabled action: retain the last valid apparatus/data state, explain the prerequisite and enable a recoverable correction; do not advance by clicking Next alone. A5. Retry clears only the current unconfirmed measurement/run. Restart experiment asks confirmation before clearing the full working attempt; retain previously saved history. A6. Missing plan values, apparatus calibration or answer key: show Assessment unavailable and do not grant a completion/unlock. AI failure leaves built-in instructions usable. |
| Pre-conditions | The workspace is available, introduction UC03.7.1 is selected, and the versioned plan/ranges/models/answer key are configured. These draft numeric choices remain subject to review. |

| Brief description | Use a microphone and oscilloscope to measure a tuning-fork period and infer its frequency. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Open this experiment introduction from Library or an accessible matching History result. 2\. Read its own objective, lesson/source status, apparatus and assumptions. Objective: Use a microphone and oscilloscope to measure a tuning-fork period and infer its frequency. 3\. Review apparatus: Tuning fork and rubber hammer, microphone and amplifier, oscilloscope and leads. 4\. Review model and limits: For a stable periodic waveform f=1/T. Horizontal time scale determines period; vertical gain changes displayed amplitude, not the physical frequency. 5\. Review available manipulation/precision controls: Drag the microphone relative to the fork, strike the fork, connect the signal path, adjust time/div and volts/div, trigger/hold the display and position period cursors. 6\. Show Back to library, primary Start Guided (UC03.8.2) and bottom-right Open Lab (UC03.8.3). Check implementation availability and this account's successful completion of this same Guided experiment; locked Open Lab stays visible with its reason. 7\. Navigate only to an available, authorized mode; otherwise keep the readable introduction with the appropriate disabled-state explanation. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. No prior success for this experiment: disable Open Lab and show Complete this Guided experiment once to unlock Open Lab; enforce the same rule for direct entry. A2. Planned/unavailable workspace: disable interactive start actions with Planned experiment even if the introduction exists. A3. Missing content/assets: identify the failed section and offer retry without claiming apparatus readiness. |
| Pre-conditions | The selected catalogue record and readable introduction exist. No account is needed merely to read this page. |
| Post-conditions | The user remains on the introduction, returns to the previous library state or enters an available authorized workspace. Reading does not grant completion/unlock. |

## **Use-case: UC03.8.2 Perform the Guided Sound Frequency experiment**

| Use case Name | UC03.8.2 \- Perform the Guided Sound Frequency experiment |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Perform the experiment using its apparatus-specific Guided procedure, direct manipulation and precision controls. Use a microphone and oscilloscope to measure a tuning-fork period and infer its frequency. This generated procedure is a draft for scientific/UX review. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Select Start Guided. Load the dedicated apparatus, display the current step and required data plan. Plan: Draft minimum three independent strikes with confirmed period readings for the assigned fork. The workbook specifies comparison with its label but no repeat count. 2\. Connect the microphone through the amplifier to the oscilloscope and place it approximately 20 cm from the tuning fork. 3\. Select AUTO trigger, strike the fork with the rubber hammer and observe the waveform. 4\. Adjust volts/div to avoid clipping and time/div so several cycles are visible; wait for a stable trigger. 5\. Hold the waveform and place two cursors on equivalent phase points. Read the time separation across one or more complete cycles and identify that cycle count. 6\. Record Δt, cycle count n and T=Δt/n, then repeat the required independent strikes rather than duplicating one saved waveform. 7\. Open Results, compare f=1/T with the fork label and submit the time-scale/frequency interpretation. 8\. At each measurement stage, validate the current preparation/measurement before enabling the next action; show live readings with units. Record trial commits an immutable row/run, and the next required stage unlocks only after confirmation. 9\. At UC03.8.4, require the complete configured plan, valid recorded data and correct experiment-specific conclusions: Period is the duration of a full cycle; frequency is its reciprocal. Horizontal and vertical oscilloscope scales control different displayed quantities. 10\. Only successful assessment marks this Guided experiment completed and unlocks its own Open Lab for the account. Show completion and return/Open Lab/export options; do not unlock a different experiment. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. Microphone disconnected, low signal/noise or clipped/unstable trace: allow gain/position/trigger adjustment and keep Record disabled. A2. Cursors select unlike phase points, n is not a positive integer or Δt≤0: explain the period selection and do not calculate f. A3. Changing time/div on a held trace must update its calibrated axis; never reinterpret the same divisions using an unrelated scale. A4. Out-of-order or disabled action: retain the last valid apparatus/data state, explain the prerequisite and enable a recoverable correction; do not advance by clicking Next alone. A5. Retry clears only the current unconfirmed measurement/run. Restart experiment asks confirmation before clearing the full working attempt; retain previously saved history. A6. 11. ## **Speed of Sound by Resonance**

## **Use-case: UC03.9.1 View Speed of Sound by Resonance introduction**

| Use case Name | UC03.9.1 \- View Speed of Sound by Resonance introduction |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Find two adjacent resonant air-column lengths at a known frequency and estimate the speed of sound. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Open this experiment introduction from Library or an accessible matching History result. 2\. Read its own objective, lesson/source status, apparatus and assumptions. Objective: Find two adjacent resonant air-column lengths at a known frequency and estimate the speed of sound. 3\. Review apparatus: Adjustable air-column tube with piston and ruler; signal generator and speaker, or a tuning fork and rubber hammer. 4\. Review model and limits: For adjacent resonances of the same supported tube mode and frequency, λ=2(l2−l1), v=2f(l2−l1). Using the difference reduces a shared end-correction offset. 5\. Review available manipulation/precision controls: Drag the piston, use precise length input/readout, adjust source frequency and amplitude, and mark observed loudness maxima. 6\. Show Back to library, primary Start Guided (UC03.9.2) and bottom-right Open Lab (UC03.9.3). Check implementation availability and this account's successful completion of this same Guided experiment; locked Open Lab stays visible with its reason. 7\. Navigate only to an available, authorized mode; otherwise keep the readable introduction with the appropriate disabled-state explanation. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. No prior success for this experiment: disable Open Lab and show Complete this Guided experiment once to unlock Open Lab; enforce the same rule for direct entry. A2. Planned/unavailable workspace: disable interactive start actions with Planned experiment even if the introduction exists. A3. Missing content/assets: identify the failed section and offer retry without claiming apparatus readiness. |
| Pre-conditions | The selected catalogue record and readable introduction exist. No account is needed merely to read this page. |
| Post-conditions | The user remains on the introduction, returns to the previous library state or enters an available authorized workspace. Reading does not grant completion/unlock. |

## **Use-case: UC03.9.2 Perform the Guided Speed of Sound by Resonance experiment**

| Use case Name | UC03.9.2 \- Perform the Guided Speed of Sound by Resonance experiment |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Perform the experiment using its apparatus-specific Guided procedure, direct manipulation and precision controls. Find two adjacent resonant air-column lengths at a known frequency and estimate the speed of sound. This generated procedure is a draft for scientific/UX review. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Select Start Guided. Load the dedicated apparatus, display the current step and required data plan. Plan: Three confirmed pairs of adjacent resonances at one assigned frequency, as stated in the workbook. 2\. Select the supported sound source, set/read its frequency f and place it at the tube opening with usable loudness. 3\. Move the piston slowly through the allowed range and listen for the first observed loudness maximum. Mark l1 using the ruler. 4\. Continue in the same direction to the next adjacent maximum without changing frequency. Mark l2. 5\. Inspect the two marked positions and source frequency, then Retry pair or Record pair. The system must not select resonance positions automatically in place of observation. 6\. Repeat until three resonance pairs are confirmed. Retain the same frequency and distinguish each independent search. 7\. Open Results, calculate wavelength and speed with uncertainty and submit the adjacent-resonance interpretation. 8\. At each measurement stage, validate the current preparation/measurement before enabling the next action; show live readings with units. Record trial commits an immutable row/run, and the next required stage unlocks only after confirmation. 9\. At UC03.9.4, require the complete configured plan, valid recorded data and correct experiment-specific conclusions: Successive resonant lengths differ by half a wavelength for the declared tube model. Frequency and wavelength together determine speed; loudness alone is not speed. 10\. Only successful assessment marks this Guided experiment completed and unlocks its own Open Lab for the account. Show completion and return/Open Lab/export options; do not unlock a different experiment. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. l2≤l1, missing frequency or a frequency changed between marks: block confirmation and request a new pair. A2. A non-adjacent resonance is selected or the source is inaudible/clipped: explain how to search again rather than silently divide by an assumed mode number. A3. Millimetre ruler readings must be converted to metres before v is calculated; retain original display units in the row metadata. A4. Out-of-order or disabled action: retain the last valid apparatus/data state, explain the prerequisite and enable a recoverable correction; do not advance by clicking Next alone. A5. Retry clears only the current unconfirmed measurement/run. Restart experiment asks confirmation before clearing the full working attempt; retain previously saved history. A6. Missing plan values, apparatus calibration or answer key: show Assessment unavailable and do not grant a completion/unlock. AI failure leaves built-in instructions usable. |
| Pre-conditions | The workspace is available, introduction UC03.9.1 is selected, and the versioned plan/ranges/models/answer key are configured. These draft numeric choices remain subject to review. |
| Post-conditions | Valid confirmed measurements and observations are available in UC03.9.4. A successful configured assessment grants only this account's experiment-specific completion/unlock; incomplete or failed work does not. Previously earned unlocks are retained. |
