12. ## **Battery EMF and Internal Resistance**

## **Use-case: UC03.10.1 View Battery EMF and Internal Resistance introduction**

| Use case Name | UC03.10.1 \- View Battery EMF and Internal Resistance introduction |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Estimate the EMF and internal resistance of an old and a new cell from terminal voltage-current graphs. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Open this experiment introduction from Library or an accessible matching History result. 2\. Read its own objective, lesson/source status, apparatus and assumptions. Objective: Estimate the EMF and internal resistance of an old and a new cell from terminal voltage-current graphs. 3\. Review apparatus: Old/new 1.5 V cells, 100 Ω rheostat, two digital multimeters, protective resistor R0, switch, wires and circuit board. 4\. Review model and limits: Terminal relation U=E−rI for the declared cell model. Graph intercept at I=0 estimates E; its negative slope estimates r. Current must be in A for resistance in Ω. 5\. Review available manipulation/precision controls: Connect terminals directly in 3D, place ammeter in series and voltmeter across the cell, drag/set the rheostat, operate the switch and select fit points on U-I graphs. 6\. Show Back to library, primary Start Guided (UC03.10.2) and bottom-right Open Lab (UC03.10.3). Check implementation availability and this account's successful completion of this same Guided experiment; locked Open Lab stays visible with its reason. 7\. Navigate only to an available, authorized mode; otherwise keep the readable introduction with the appropriate disabled-state explanation. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. No prior success for this experiment: disable Open Lab and show Complete this Guided experiment once to unlock Open Lab; enforce the same rule for direct entry. A2. Planned/unavailable workspace: disable interactive start actions with Planned experiment even if the introduction exists. A3. Missing content/assets: identify the failed section and offer retry without claiming apparatus readiness. |
| Pre-conditions | The selected catalogue record and readable introduction exist. No account is needed merely to read this page. |
| Post-conditions | The user remains on the introduction, returns to the previous library state or enters an available authorized workspace. Reading does not grant completion/unlock. |

## **Use-case: UC03.10.2 Perform the Guided Battery EMF and Internal Resistance experiment**

| Use case Name | UC03.10.2 \- Perform the Guided Battery EMF and Internal Resistance experiment |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Perform the experiment using its apparatus-specific Guided procedure, direct manipulation and precision controls. Estimate the EMF and internal resistance of an old and a new cell from terminal voltage-current graphs. This generated procedure is a draft for scientific/UX review. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Select Start Guided. Load the dedicated apparatus, display the current step and required data plan. Plan: Five decreasing-rheostat measurements for the old cell, starting at 100 Ω, then five for the new cell; workbook and local Bài 26 transcript agree on the count. 2\. Select the old cell, connect the protective resistor and meters correctly and set the rheostat to 100 Ω while the switch is open. 3\. Validate meter mode/range, polarity, complete circuit and protective load before enabling the switch. 4\. Close the switch, wait for a valid reading, record terminal U and I, then open the switch. Keep the loaded interval short. 5\. Decrease the rheostat as assigned and repeat four further times, yielding five distinct-current snapshots for the old cell. 6\. With the switch open, replace the cell with the new cell and restore the rheostat to 100 Ω. Repeat the five-measurement series. 7\. Open Results, fit U against I for each cell and extend the fitted line to its U-axis intercept. 8\. Choose two distinct-current points on each fitted line, calculate r and inspect graph-based uncertainty; submit the cell-model conclusions. 9\. At each measurement stage, validate the current preparation/measurement before enabling the next action; show live readings with units. Record trial commits an immutable row/run, and the next required stage unlocks only after confirmation. 10\. At UC03.10.4, require the complete configured plan, valid recorded data and correct experiment-specific conclusions: Terminal voltage under load differs from EMF in the internal-resistance model. Intercept and slope encode different parameters. The data, rather than the old/new label, determine their estimated values. 11\. Only successful assessment marks this Guided experiment completed and unlocks its own Open Lab for the account. Show completion and return/Open Lab/export options; do not unlock a different experiment. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. Short circuit, missing protective resistor, incorrect meter wiring/range or an excessive current: refuse switch closure and highlight the required fix. A2. Changing cell/wires while loaded: request switch open first; never alter already recorded rows. A3. Fit has fewer than two distinct currents or selected fit points share I: do not calculate slope/r; keep the raw table. A4. A nonnegative fitted U-I slope is inconsistent with the stated positive-r model; flag the data/model issue rather than silently forcing a positive r. A5. Out-of-order or disabled action: retain the last valid apparatus/data state, explain the prerequisite and enable a recoverable correction; do not advance by clicking Next alone. A6. Retry clears only the current unconfirmed measurement/run. Restart experiment asks confirmation before clearing the full working attempt; retain previously saved history. A7. Missing plan values, apparatus calibration or answer key: show Assessment unavailable and do not grant a completion/unlock. AI failure leaves built-in instructions usable. |
| Pre-conditions | The workspace is available, introduction UC03.10.1 is selected, and the versioned plan/ranges/models/answer key are configured. These draft numeric choices remain subject to review. |
| Post-conditions | Valid confirmed measurements and observations are available in UC03.10.4. A successful configured assessment grants only this account's experiment-specific completion/unlock; incomplete or failed work does not. Previously earned unlocks are retained. |

Review apparatus: Water calorimeter, electrical heater, thermometer, power measurement/timer and a measured or supplied water mass with its provenance. 4\. Review model and limits: Under declared negligible heat loss and calorimeter/heater heat-capacity assumptions, c=Q/(mΔθ), with Q=P\_average Δt for approximately constant measured power. 5\. Review available manipulation/precision controls: Add/set water mass, place the probe and heater, switch heating, read power/time/temperature, and position analysis cursors on the temperature-time graph. 6\. Show Back to library, primary Start Guided (UC03.11.2) and bottom-right Open Lab (UC03.11.3). ## **Use-case: UC03.11.2 Perform the Guided Specific Heat Capacity of Water experiment**

| Use case Name | UC03.11.2 \- Perform the Guided Specific Heat Capacity of Water experiment |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Perform the experiment using its apparatus-specific Guided procedure, direct manipulation and precision controls. Estimate water specific heat capacity from electrical heating, mass and the temperature-time change. This generated procedure is a draft for scientific/UX review. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Select Start Guided. Load the dedicated apparatus, display the current step and required data plan. Plan: Draft: one adequately sampled heating run with at least five confirmed time-temperature-power samples, plus a second mass/run for comparison. The workbook does not fix the sample or run count. 2\. Provide the measured water mass in kg with its measurement source. Place the heater and probe in the water and declare the thermal-model assumptions. 3\. Check that the water covers the heater, power is within the supported range and initial temperature is available. 4\. Start the power/timer together and observe temperature and actual power. Record the configured time-temperature-power samples while heating remains below boiling. 5\. Stop heating and confirm the run, preserving the sampled trace and instrument metadata. Retry affects only the unconfirmed run. 6\. Repeat the configured comparison run with its own water mass and initial state. 7\. Open Results, fit the eligible heating segment and select two analysis points M/N. Calculate c, discuss uncertainty/heat losses and submit conclusions. 8\. At each measurement stage, validate the current preparation/measurement before enabling the next action; show live readings with units. Record trial commits an immutable row/run, and the next required stage unlocks only after confirmation. 9\. At UC03.11.4, require the complete configured plan, valid recorded data and correct experiment-specific conclusions: Energy required for a temperature rise depends on mass and specific heat. Electrical input equals water heat gain only under the declared energy-loss assumptions. 10\. Only successful assessment marks this Guided experiment completed and unlocks its own Open Lab for the account. Show completion and return/Open Lab/export options; do not unlock a different experiment. |

Review apparatus: Water calorimeter, electrical heater, power/timer and balance measuring the water/apparatus mass. 4\. Review available manipulation/precision controls: Set water quantity, tare/read the balance, place heater, start/stop heating, monitor boiling, and position two graph cursors on mass-time data. 6\. ## **Use-case: UC03.13.2 Perform the Guided Specific Latent Heat of Vaporization of Water experiment**

| Use case Name | UC03.13.2 \- Perform the Guided Specific Latent Heat of Vaporization of Water experiment |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Perform the experiment using its apparatus-specific Guided procedure, direct manipulation and precision controls. Estimate water vaporization energy per unit mass from steady-boiling mass loss and electrical energy. This generated procedure is a draft for scientific/UX review. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Select Start Guided. Load the dedicated apparatus, display the current step and required data plan. Plan: Draft: one steady-boiling run with at least five confirmed mass-time-power samples and one selected positive-mass-loss interval. The workbook does not state a mandatory repeat count. 2\. Prepare water and heater, identify the balance tare/apparatus mass, and confirm that enough water covers the heater. 3\. Begin heating and observe the warm-up. Do not count warm-up energy as vaporization energy. 4\. When steady boiling is established, start the recorded analysis interval and collect mass, time and actual power samples. 5\. Stop heating before the heater becomes exposed, and confirm the mass-time trace and boiling-state metadata. 6\. Open Results, fit the steady-boiling mass-loss line and select points P/Q with later time and lower mass. 7\. Calculate L from input energy and lost water mass, inspect uncertainty/heat losses and submit the phase-change conclusions. 8\. At each measurement stage, validate the current preparation/measurement before enabling the next action; show live readings with units. Record trial commits an immutable row/run, and the next required stage unlocks only after confirmation. 9\. At UC03.13.4, require the complete configured plan, valid recorded data and correct experiment-specific conclusions: Vaporization energy is associated with mass changing phase. A steady boiling temperature does not imply zero energy input. Warm-up and vaporization require different energy accounting. 10\. Only successful assessment marks this Guided experiment completed and unlocks its own Open Lab for the account. Show completion and return/Open Lab/export options; do not unlock a different experiment. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. Balance not tared, heater not submerged or water insufficient: keep heating/recording unavailable as appropriate and show the correction. A2. Selected interval includes warm-up, splashing/spillage or missing mass readings: exclude it from the simple vaporization estimate and explain the cause. A3. Δm≤0 or Δt≤0: do not divide or produce a numerical L; retain the trace and allow a new interval. A4. Out-of-order or disabled action: retain the last valid apparatus/data state, explain the prerequisite and enable a recoverable correction; do not advance by clicking Next alone. A5. Retry clears only the current unconfirmed measurement/run. Restart experiment asks confirmation before clearing the full working attempt; retain previously saved history. A6. Missing plan values, apparatus calibration or answer key: show Assessment unavailable and do not grant a completion/unlock. AI failure leaves built-in instructions usable. |

## **Use-case: UC03.14.2 Perform the Guided Boyle Law experiment**

| Use case Name | UC03.14.2 \- Perform the Guided Boyle Law experiment |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Perform the experiment using its apparatus-specific Guided procedure, direct manipulation and precision controls. Investigate pressure-volume dependence for a fixed amount of gas at approximately constant temperature. This generated procedure is a draft for scientific/UX review. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Select Start Guided. Load the dedicated apparatus, display the current step and required data plan. Plan: Draft minimum five distinct settled volumes for a single sealed gas sample. The workbook lists a p-V table without a fixed point count. 2\. Assemble and seal the cylinder, declare the fixed gas sample and identify whether the gauge reports absolute or relative pressure. 3\. Read the initial volume, pressure and thermal-equilibrium state. Convert all calculation quantities to SI. 4\. Move the piston slowly to the first assigned volume and wait for temperature to return to the declared reference condition. 5\. Record the settled pressure-volume pair. Repeat for the configured distinct volumes without opening the gas container. 6\. Confirm the series and open Results to inspect pV and the p-versus-1/V relation. 7\. Submit conclusions about inverse proportionality and the roles of fixed temperature and gas amount. 8\. At each measurement stage, validate the current preparation/measurement before enabling the next action; show live readings with units. Record trial commits an immutable row/run, and the next required stage unlocks only after confirmation. 9\. | Use case Name | UC03.15.1 \- View Charles Law introduction |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Investigate gas volume versus absolute temperature at approximately constant pressure. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Open this experiment introduction from Library or an accessible matching History result. 2\. Read its own objective, lesson/source status, apparatus and assumptions. Objective: Investigate gas volume versus absolute temperature at approximately constant pressure. 3\. Review apparatus: 50 mL graduated cylinder with free piston and sealed gas, thermometer, support, lubricant and room-temperature/ice/warm/hot water baths. 4\. Review model and limits: For fixed gas amount and constant pressure, V/T\_kelvin is constant. Use T(K)=θ(°C)+273.15; the workbook uses the rounded teaching conversion \+273. 5\. Review available manipulation/precision controls: Drag the cylinder between baths, lubricate/unlock the piston, read/set initial volume, inspect piston freedom and temperature, and record settled states. 6\. Show Back to library, primary Start Guided (UC03.15.2) and bottom-right Open Lab (UC03.15.3). Check implementation availability and this account's successful completion of this same Guided experiment; locked Open Lab stays visible with its reason. 7\. Navigate only to an available, authorized mode; otherwise keep the readable introduction with the appropriate disabled-state explanation. |

Missing content/assets: identify the failed section and offer retry without claiming apparatus readiness. |

## **Use-case: UC03.15.2 Perform the Guided Charles Law experiment**

| Use case Name | UC03.15.2 \- Perform the Guided Charles Law experiment |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Perform the experiment using its apparatus-specific Guided procedure, direct manipulation and precision controls. Investigate gas volume versus absolute temperature at approximately constant pressure. This generated procedure is a draft for scientific/UX review. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Select Start Guided. Load the dedicated apparatus, display the current step and required data plan. Plan: Initial room-temperature state near the source 30 mL setup, then ice, warm and hot baths: four equilibrium states. The source suggests about 3 minutes in the ice bath; equilibrium, not elapsed wall time alone, validates a reading. 2\. Lubricate the piston, set the initial gas volume to 30 mL, seal the gas and leave the piston free to move under the declared constant external load. 3\. Read the initial room-temperature gas volume and temperature after equilibrium, and record the baseline state. 4\. Place the cylinder in the ice-water bath. Wait for gas/bath equilibration, with elapsed simulated time shown, then record V and θ. 5\. Repeat equilibration and recording in the warm-water and hot-water baths without changing gas amount or piston load. 6\. Confirm the four-state series and open Results. Convert Celsius readings to Kelvin before computing V/T. 7\. Compare the V-T fit and ratio constancy, then submit the constant-pressure/absolute-temperature conclusion. 8\. At each measurement stage, validate the current preparation/measurement before enabling the next action; show live readings with units. Record trial commits an immutable row/run, and the next required stage unlocks only after confirmation. 9\. At UC03.15.4, require the complete configured plan, valid recorded data and correct experiment-specific conclusions: At constant pressure, volume is proportional to absolute temperature. A free piston supports the pressure condition; a locked piston changes the experiment. 10\. Only successful assessment marks this Guided experiment completed and unlocks its own Open Lab for the account. Show completion and return/Open Lab/export options; do not unlock a different experiment. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. Piston stuck/locked or external load changed: the constant-pressure condition is not established; disable recording for this series until corrected. A2. Thermometer/gas not equilibrated: show Wait for thermal equilibrium and retain prior valid records. A3. Leak, unsupported bath temperature, V outside cylinder range or T≤0 K: reject the state or start a new gas series. Never calculate V/θ using Celsius. A4. Out-of-order or disabled action: retain the last valid apparatus/data state, explain the prerequisite and enable a recoverable correction; do not advance by clicking Next alone. A5. Retry clears only the current unconfirmed measurement/run. Restart experiment asks confirmation before clearing the full working attempt; retain previously saved history. A6. Missing plan values, apparatus calibration or answer key: show Assessment unavailable and do not grant a completion/unlock. AI failure leaves built-in instructions usable. |
| Pre-conditions | The workspace is available, introduction UC03.15.1 is selected, and the versioned plan/ranges/models/answer key are configured. These draft numeric choices remain subject to review. |

18. ## **Magnetic Flux Density from Force**

## **Use-case: UC03.16.1 View Magnetic Flux Density from Force introduction**

| Use case Name | UC03.16.1 \- View Magnetic Flux Density from Force introduction |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Measure force on a current-carrying conductor in a magnetic field and infer B with a declared active conductor length. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Open this experiment introduction from Library or an accessible matching History result. 2\. Read its own objective, lesson/source status, apparatus and assumptions. Objective: Measure force on a current-carrying conductor in a magnetic field and infer B with a declared active conductor length. 3\. Review apparatus: Electromagnet, frame/lever and counterweight, force meter, two ammeters/rheostats, reversing switches, field-direction indicator and DC supplies; source frame: 200 turns with a 10 cm edge. 4\. Review model and limits: F=B I L\_effective sin α for the measured active conductors. If N equivalent turns each contribute one active length ℓ in the same field, L\_effective=Nℓ; never multiply by N again after using total active length. 5\. Review available manipulation/precision controls: Position the frame in the pole gap, tare the force meter/lever, set frame angle and currents precisely, reverse current and read signed force/field orientation. 6\. Show Back to library, primary Start Guided (UC03.16.2) and bottom-right Open Lab (UC03.16.3). Check implementation availability and this account's successful completion of this same Guided experiment; locked Open Lab stays visible with its reason. 7\. Navigate only to an available, authorized mode; otherwise keep the readable introduction with the appropriate disabled-state explanation. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. No prior success for this experiment: disable Open Lab and show Complete this Guided experiment once to unlock Open Lab; enforce the same rule for direct entry. A2. Planned/unavailable workspace: disable interactive start actions with Planned experiment even if the introduction exists. A3. Missing content/assets: identify the failed section and offer retry without claiming apparatus readiness. |
| Pre-conditions | The selected catalogue record and readable introduction exist. No account is needed merely to read this page. |
| Post-conditions | The user remains on the introduction, returns to the previous library state or enters an available authorized workspace. Reading does not grant completion/unlock. |

## **Use-case: UC03.16.2 Perform the Guided Magnetic Flux Density from Force experiment**

| Use case Name | UC03.16.2 \- Perform the Guided Magnetic Flux Density from Force experiment |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Perform the experiment using its apparatus-specific Guided procedure, direct manipulation and precision controls. Measure force on a current-carrying conductor in a magnetic field and infer B with a declared active conductor length. This generated procedure is a draft for scientific/UX review. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Select Start Guided. Load the dedicated apparatus, display the current step and required data plan. Plan: Draft: direction checks for forward/reversed frame current and at least five positive-current force readings at α=90° with field current fixed. Point count is proposed, not specified in the workbook. 2\. Assemble the electromagnet and measuring circuit, balance/tare the force apparatus and record the active-length/turn-count calibration. 3\. Place the active frame section in the uniform field region and set α=90°. Establish and retain a fixed electromagnet current. 4\. Close the frame-current circuit at an allowed value and observe the force direction; reverse that current and record the changed direction. 5\. Return to the assigned polarity, vary frame current through the configured settings and record settled force and current snapshots. 6\. Open both circuits after measurement and confirm the series, retaining angle, current polarity, active length and tare metadata. 7\. Open Results, inspect F-I and calculate B only when the effective active length is unambiguous. Submit magnitude/direction conclusions. 8\. At each measurement stage, validate the current preparation/measurement before enabling the next action; show live readings with units. Record trial commits an immutable row/run, and the next required stage unlocks only after confirmation. 9\. At UC03.16.4, require the complete configured plan, valid recorded data and correct experiment-specific conclusions: Force direction changes when the current reverses. Magnitude depends on I, B, active length and sin α. A multi-turn frame requires the correct effective length. 10\. Only successful assessment marks this Guided experiment completed and unlocks its own Open Lab for the account. Show completion and return/Open Lab/export options; do not unlock a different experiment. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. Untared force meter, frame outside the declared uniform region or invalid current wiring/range: block recording and identify the correction. A2. Electromagnet current changes during a frame-current series: separate the changed-field data rather than fitting one constant-B line. A3. I=0, sin α=0 or active length/turn participation unresolved: allow a labeled qualitative force observation but show B as undefined/unavailable. A4. The source gives 200 turns: do not treat both Nℓ and a second factor N as active length. Require the calibration metadata before numerical inference. A5. Out-of-order or disabled action: retain the last valid apparatus/data state, explain the prerequisite and enable a recoverable correction; do not advance by clicking Next alone. A6. Retry clears only the current unconfirmed measurement/run. Restart experiment asks confirmation before clearing the full working attempt; retain previously saved history. A7. Missing plan values, apparatus calibration or answer key: show Assessment unavailable and do not grant a completion/unlock. AI failure leaves built-in instructions usable. |

| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Select Start Guided. Load the dedicated apparatus, display the current step and required data plan. Plan: Magnet approach/withdrawal/stationary observations, then primary switch close/open and increasing/decreasing-current observations: seven labeled event types; this event set is a proposed explicit expansion of the workbook steps. 2\. Connect and zero the galvanometer, identify coil winding/terminal orientation and label the approaching magnet pole. 3\. Move that pole toward the coil, record needle direction and motion condition, then move it away and record the reversed-motion observation. 4\. Hold the magnet stationary and record the settled needle response, keeping transient and steady observations distinct. 5\. Select the electromagnet setup, connect primary/secondary circuits and declare their relative orientation. Check galvanometer zero again. 6\. Close and open the primary switch separately; capture the galvanometer transient associated with each event. 7\. With the primary circuit closed, increase and decrease rheostat-controlled current and record the corresponding transient directions. 8\. Open the primary circuit and confirm the observation log. Compare events in Results and submit the flux-change/Lenz conclusions. 9\. At each measurement stage, validate the current preparation/measurement before enabling the next action; show live readings with units. Record trial commits an immutable row/run, and the next required stage unlocks only after confirmation. 10\. At UC03.17.4, require the complete configured plan, valid recorded data and correct experiment-specific conclusions: A changing linked flux produces the observed transient; a steady field alone does not sustain it in this setup. Reversing the relevant flux change reverses response with orientation fixed, consistent with Lenz law. 11\. Only successful assessment marks this Guided experiment completed and unlocks its own Open Lab for the account. Show completion and return/Open Lab/export options; do not unlock a different experiment. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. Galvanometer not zeroed, leads disconnected or primary wiring invalid: disable the affected event capture and highlight the apparatus issue. A2. Stationary response is mistaken for a missed transient: preserve event time and settling state, and allow a fresh event recording. A3. Unknown winding/terminal orientation: do not mark absolute left/right needle claims correct; require orientation or restrict the comparison to reversal relationships. A4. No calibrated current/EMF sensor: display needle deflection in divisions or direction only, never fabricate numerical amperes, volts or a percentage error. A5. Out-of-order or disabled action: retain the last valid apparatus/data state, explain the prerequisite and enable a recoverable correction; do not advance by clicking Next alone. A6. Retry clears only the current unconfirmed measurement/run. Restart experiment asks confirmation before clearing the full working attempt; retain previously saved history. A7. Missing plan values, apparatus calibration or answer key: show Assessment unavailable and do not grant a completion/unlock. AI failure leaves built-in instructions usable. |
| Pre-conditions | The workspace is available, introduction UC03.17.1 is selected, and the versioned plan/ranges/models/answer key are configured. These draft numeric choices remain subject to review. |
| Post-conditions | Valid confirmed measurements and observations are available in UC03.17.4. A successful configured assessment grants only this account's experiment-specific completion/unlock; incomplete or failed work does not. Previously earned unlocks are retained. |

## **Use-case: UC03.17.3 Perform the Electromagnetic Induction experiment in Open Lab**

| Use case Name | UC03.17.3 \- Perform the Electromagnetic Induction experiment in Open Lab |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Use the same Electromagnetic Induction apparatus without step-by-step guidance or the Guided plan/checklist. Keep physical validity, access and data-preservation rules. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Enter the bottom-right Open Lab option from this introduction or successful Guided result; check availability and the account's unlock for this exact experiment. 2\. Load the same supported workspace with live measurements and no Guided step progression. Apparatus: Bar magnet, coil and galvanometer/leads; second setup uses electromagnet/primary coil, source, switch, rheostat and a secondary coil/galvanometer. 3\. Operate supported controls freely between measurements: Drag the magnet toward/away from the coil, orient its pole, connect galvanometer terminals and zero it; operate primary switch/rheostat and record needle events. 4\. Explore: Explore either setup in arbitrary order, change supported magnet motion/polarity or primary current, and record qualitative events with their orientations and conditions. 5\. Use Record trial to commit valid snapshots or Retry to discard the current provisional reading/run. Parameter/model changes create separately identified conditions/series rather than modifying recorded data. 6\. Open UC03.17.4 for analysis/comparison or UC08 for report export. Exit and experiment restart follow the shared confirmation/data-preservation rules. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. No same-experiment unlock or unavailable workspace: prevent entry and explain the applicable restriction. A2. Galvanometer not zeroed, leads disconnected or primary wiring invalid: disable the affected event capture and highlight the apparatus issue. A3. Stationary response is mistaken for a missed transient: preserve event time and settling state, and allow a fresh event recording. A4. Unknown winding/terminal orientation: do not mark absolute left/right needle claims correct; require orientation or restrict the comparison to reversal relationships. A5. No calibrated current/EMF sensor: display needle deflection in divisions or direction only, never fabricate numerical amperes, volts or a percentage error. A6. Changing a configuration during active acquisition invalidates only the affected unconfirmed measurement when that change would break its model; require stop/reprepare first. A7. No Guided checklist, required trial count, completion questions or unlock effect applies. Open Lab history retention follows the pending shared policy. |
| Pre-conditions | This account has passed UC03.17.2 at least once and its supported workspace is available. Scientific model/ranges and calibration metadata are defined. |
| Post-conditions | Supported self-selected measurements/observations are recorded with Open Lab mode labels. Existing unlocks are unchanged; no new Guided completion is created. |

## **Use-case: UC03.17.4 View and compare Electromagnetic Induction results**

| Use case Name | UC03.17.4 \- View and compare Electromagnetic Induction results |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | Show the actual experiment-specific results from Guided or Open Lab, including recorded tables, relevant graphs and compatible multi-trial/condition comparisons. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | 1\. Load confirmed snapshots from the current workspace or accessible saved result. Keep session, experiment, branch, configuration and mode labels; provisional readings remain distinct. 2\. Present these data fields with units: Event; mode; setup; pole/coil/terminal orientation; magnet motion or primary switch/current change; event time (s); needle direction; peak/settled deflection (divisions if available); user observation. 3\. Derive analysis from those same raw records: Compare induced-response directions for approach/withdrawal and switch close/open with orientation fixed. Distinguish transient response from steady state. Faraday/Lenz formulas are explanatory models; no numerical EMF is inferred from an uncalibrated needle. 4\. Show graphs: Galvanometer deflection versus time in instrument divisions, with labeled event markers; an observation comparison matrix for paired events. 5\. Allow compatible selection/comparison: Compare events only with the orientation/pole/terminal metadata shown. Reverse a declared condition explicitly; do not pool incompatible left/right conventions. 6\. For Guided, show the configured plan progress and collect the specific conclusion response(s): A changing linked flux produces the observed transient; a steady field alone does not sustain it in this setup. Reversing the relevant flux change reverses response with orientation fixed, consistent with Lenz law. All required data and all configured conclusion responses must be valid/correct before successful completion. 7\. For Open Lab, show its available data/analysis without the Guided checklist or completion effect. Offer Export report (UC08) from the same source snapshots; templates include this experiment's quantities and assumptions. |
| Alternative Flows | A1. No confirmed data: show an empty state without invented rows or a completion badge. A2. Missing denominator, calibration, model condition or insufficient fit points: keep raw data and show the derived value/fit as unavailable with a reason. A3. Incompatible units, branches or conditions: explain the comparison restriction and keep the selected records intact. A4. Graph rendering fails: retain the labeled table and retry; never substitute theoretical values as measured samples. A5. Incomplete plan/conclusions cannot pass. Proposed new-experiment conclusions may be reviewed/corrected; raw confirmed measurements remain immutable. The Pendulum restart-only failure policy is not implied. A6. Saved history is read-only. Export/AI errors do not change the observations, completion or unlock. |
| Pre-conditions | A matching current or saved result is selected. Numerical analysis requires its declared physical inputs; Guided assessment additionally requires the configured plan and answer key. |
| Post-conditions | The experiment-specific result, graph/comparison or explicit unavailable state is shown without rewriting records. Only successful configured Guided assessment grants the corresponding account unlock. |

20. ## **Use-case: UC05 Ask the AI lab assistant**

| Use case Name | UC05 – Ask the AI lab assistant |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | This use case describes how a user asks the AI lab assistant for help during a guided experiment or in Open Lab. The assistant knows the current experiment, step, settings and readings. It explains the current step, an apparatus or a result, and suggests how to fix a mistake. A setting it suggests is changed only after the user confirms and only if the value is allowed. The assistant does not create new experiments or invent measurements, and the experiment keeps working when the assistant is unavailable. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) – primary actor AI Service – secondary actor (external system that generates the answers) |
| Basic Flow |  In a Guided experiment (UC03.n.2) or an authorized Open Lab workspace (UC03.n.3), the user selects Ask AI when the current experiment state allows the panel to be opened The system opens the AI panel and displays the selected experiment, mode and current Guided step when applicable. It provides suggested questions relevant to the current context, a question box and the Send button. In Open Lab, the context describes the current configuration and activity without inventing a Guided step. The user types a question of up to 500 characters, or clicks a suggested question, and then clicks ‘Send’. The system shows the question in the panel and, within 200 ms, a loading indicator. The system sends the question to the AI Service together with the selected experiment, mode, current Guided step when applicable, current settings, readings and recorded trials. Personal account information is not included in the experiment context. The AI Service returns an answer. Within 15 seconds of step 3, the system displays the answer in the language of the interface (Vietnamese or English). Any value from the experiment is quoted exactly as the instruments show it. Under each answer, the system shows the note “AI answers can be wrong. Check with your textbook or teacher.” The user reads the answer and may ask a follow-up question by repeating steps 3–7. The conversation stays in the panel until the user leaves the experiment. The user clicks ‘Close’; the system closes the panel, and the experiment continues from the same state. |
| Alternative Flows | **Alternative flow 1: The assistant suggests changing a setting** From step 7 of the basic flow, if the answer suggests a new value for a setting, the system shows a box under the answer, for example “Suggested change: Source voltage 3.0 V → 6.0 V”, with the buttons ‘Apply’ and ‘Ignore’. If the user selects Apply, the system revalidates the suggested change against the selected experiment’s supported actions, parameter limits, current state, Guided step when applicable and access restrictions. It applies the change only if all checks pass and displays “Change applied.” Otherwise, it retains the current state and explains why the change cannot be applied. Confirmed trial records are never modified.  If the value is not allowed, the system does not apply it and shows “This change cannot be applied: \<reason\>“, for example “Allowed range: 0.0–12.0 V”. If the user clicks ‘Ignore’, nothing changes. Continue at step 8 of the basic flow. **Alternative flow 2: The question is outside the current experiment** From step 6 of the basic flow, if the question is not about the current experiment or the physics behind it (for example, homework in another subject, a personal question, or a request to create a new experiment), the answer explains that the assistant can only help with the current experiment and lists what it can help with. Continue at step 8 of the basic flow. **Alternative flow 3: The user asks for results that have not been measured** From step 6 of the basic flow, if the user asks the assistant to give measurement values or to complete the experiment for them, the answer explains that it cannot provide values that have not been measured, and describes how to measure them. Continue at step 8 of the basic flow. **Alternative flow 4: The AI Service does not respond** From step 6 of the basic flow, if the AI Service returns an error or does not respond within 15 seconds, the system displays “The AI lab assistant is not available right now. Your experiment is not affected.” with the button ‘Try again’. The step instructions, the ‘Hint’ links and all experiment controls continue to work. The user clicks ‘Try again’ (continue at step 4 of the basic flow) or ‘Close’. **Alternative flow 5: There is no internet connection** From step 1 of the basic flow, if the computer is offline, the system shows the ‘Ask AI’ button as ‘AI offline’ and disables the question box, with the message “Connect to the internet to use the AI lab assistant.” When the connection returns, the system enables the button again. **Alternative flow 6: The question is empty or too long** From step 3 of the basic flow, ‘Send’ is disabled while the question box is empty. If the question exceeds 500 characters, the character counter turns red and the system shows “Questions are limited to 500 characters.” **Alternative flow 7: The question limit is reached** From step 3 of the basic flow, if the user has already sent 30 questions in the last hour, the system does not send the question and displays “You have reached the limit of 30 questions per hour. You can ask again at 14:35.” **Alternative flow 8: The user thinks the answer is wrong** From step 7 of the basic flow, the user clicks ‘Report this answer’ under an answer. The system performs UC09 Report a content error, with the type ‘Wrong AI answer’ and the question and answer attached. **Alternative flow 9: AI interaction is temporarily locked**  In the Guided Simple Pendulum experiment, from bob release until Stop timer, the user cannot open or interact with the AI panel. If the panel is already open, question submission and Apply controls are unavailable during this interval. A pending AI response cannot trigger an experiment change or interrupt the measurement. AI interaction becomes available again when permitted by the experiment state.  |
| Pre-conditions | A supported Guided experiment (UC03.n.2) or authorized Open Lab workspace (UC03.n.3) is open, and its current state permits AI interaction. UC05 adds no sign-in requirement beyond the access conditions of the selected mode. A working network connection is required for external AI requests; built-in instructions and the experiment remain usable when the AI Service is unavailable.  |
| Post-conditions | The answer is displayed in the AI panel. A setting is changed only if the user clicked ‘Apply’ and the value passed the checks. Recorded trials are never changed by the assistant. If the assistant is unavailable, the experiment continues without any change. |

    21. ## **Use-case: UC07 View experiment history**

| Use case Name | UC07 – View experiment history |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | This use case describes how a signed-in user sees the list of guided experiments they have carried out, opens an entry to review its data table and graph, and deletes entries they no longer need. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher), signed in |
| Basic Flow | The signed-in user clicks their name in the top-right corner and then ‘History’. The system displays the History page, with entries ordered from newest to oldest, 20 per page. Each entry shows the experiment name, the grade and lesson, the date and time, the number of trials, and the status ‘Finished’ or ‘Not finished’. The user may filter the list by grade or status, or search by experiment name; the system updates the list. The user clicks an entry. The system displays the saved result: the experiment name, lesson, date and experiment version; the settings of each trial, the data table and the graph, with the same graph views as in the experiment-specific Results use case (UC03.n.4, n=1..17); the buttons ‘Export report’ (UC08), ‘Open this experiment’ (opens its introduction page, the corresponding experiment Introduction use case), ‘Delete’ and ‘Back to history’. The saved result is read-only. The user may tick two or more trials and click ‘Compare’; the system shows the comparison view as in the comparison flow of the corresponding Results use case. The user clicks ‘Back to history’; the system returns to the list with the same filter. |
| Alternative Flows | **Alternative flow 1: The user is not signed in** From step 1 of the basic flow, the ‘History’ menu item is not shown to guests. If a guest opens the History page through a saved link, the system displays “Sign in to see your experiment history.” with the button ‘Sign in’ (UC01). After signing in, continue at step 2 of the basic flow. **Alternative flow 2: There is no history yet** From step 2 of the basic flow, if the user has no saved experiments, the system displays “You have not carried out any experiments yet.” with the button ‘Go to library’ (UC02). **Alternative flow 3: No entry matches the filter** From step 3 of the basic flow, the system displays “No experiments match this filter.” with the button ‘Clear filter’. **Alternative flow 4: The user deletes an entry** From step 5 of the basic flow, the user clicks ‘Delete’. The system asks “Delete this experiment and all its trials from your history? This cannot be undone.” with the buttons ‘Cancel’ and ‘Delete’. If the user clicks ‘Delete’, the system permanently deletes the entry and its trials and returns to the list. **Alternative flow 5: The experiment has been updated since the entry was saved** From step 5 of the basic flow, if the experiment has been changed since the trials were recorded (for example, after a content error was corrected), the system shows the note “These results were recorded with an earlier version of this experiment (version 1.0).” The saved values are not changed. **Alternative flow 6: The history cannot be loaded** From step 2 or 5 of the basic flow, if the data cannot be loaded, the system displays “Your history could not be loaded. Check your internet connection and try again.” with the button ‘Try again’. |
| Pre-conditions | The user is signed in (UC01) and the computer is connected to the internet. |
| Post-conditions | The user has viewed their saved experiments and results. Nothing is changed unless the user deleted an entry, in which case the entry and its trials are permanently removed from the account. |

    22. ## **Use-case: UC08 Export experiment results as PDF**

| Use case Name | UC08 – Export experiment results as PDF |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | The user opens a separate export page and downloads a PDF report containing selected confirmed measurements or observations from a Guided or Open Lab experiment. The report uses the same underlying recorded data as the corresponding experiment Results use case and an experiment-specific report template.  |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher) |
| Basic Flow | From an experiment Results page or an accessible History result, the user selects Export report when that action is available. The system opens the export page and identifies the experiment, source session, mode and available confirmed records. The user selects the records and report sections to include, such as objectives, assumptions, apparatus, settings, tables, graphs, comparisons and user-provided observations. The system displays a report preview and a filename ending in .pdf. The user selects Download PDF. The system generates the PDF report from the same underlying snapshots used by UC03.n.4. It preserves units, experimental conditions and Guided/Open Lab mode labels without inventing measurements or conclusions. The browser downloads the PDF report. Exporting does not change recorded data, Guided completion or Open Lab access |
| Alternative Flows | **A1. No confirmed records:**  Disable Download PDF and explain that at least one confirmed measurement or observation is required.  **A2. No records or report sections selected:**  Keep Download PDF disabled and explain the required selection.  **A3. Report generation or download fails:**  Retain the user’s selections, display the failure and offer Try again. **A4. The user cancels:**  Return to the source Results or History page without changing recorded data.  **A5. An optional section has no available data:**  Omit it or identify it as unavailable. Do not invent measurements, observations, graphs or completion information.  |
| Pre-conditions | At least one confirmed measurement or observation is available in the current Guided/Open Lab result or an accessible History result. The source result allows export. Access to a History result requires the appropriate signed-in account.  |
| Post-conditions | A PDF report is offered for download, or a recoverable error is displayed. Recorded snapshots, completion status and Open Lab permissions remain unchanged. |

    23. ## **Use-case: UC09 Report a content error**

| Use case Name | UC09 – Report a content error |
| :---- | :---- |
| Brief description | This use case describes how a signed-in user reports a suspected error in an experiment, in Open Lab or in an AI answer, for example a wrong formula, unit, reading, instruction or translation. PhysiX attaches the experiment, step, settings and readings automatically. Phy6 and the physics content expert review every report, and the user is informed of the result by email. |
| Actors | User (Student or Physics Teacher), signed in |
| Basic Flow | In a guided experiment, on an experiment’s introduction page, on a result summary or in Open Lab, the user clicks ‘Report an error’ (the flag icon in the top-right corner). The system displays the Report form with: the information attached automatically and shown read-only, for example “Experiment: Ohm’s Law (version 1.0) – Step 5 of 7 – R \= 20 Ω, U \= 6.0 V, I \= 300 mA”; the list ‘Type of error’: Wrong formula or calculation, Wrong unit, Wrong reading or graph, Unclear or wrong instruction, Wrong translation, Wrong AI answer, Other; the field ‘Description’ (20–1000 characters); the check box ‘Attach a picture of the current screen’, ticked by default; the buttons ‘Send report’ and ‘Cancel’. The user selects the type of error, describes the problem and clicks ‘Send report’. The system checks that a type is selected and that the description has 20-1000 characters. The system saves the report with the attached information and the status ‘Received’, and displays “Thank you. Your report number is R-2026-0153. We will email you the result within 7 working days.” The system closes the form; the experiment or Open Lab continues unchanged. |
| Alternative Flows | **Alternative flow 1: The user is not signed in** From step 1 of the basic flow, if the user is a guest, the system displays “Sign in to report an error.” with the buttons ‘Sign in’ and ‘Cancel’. If the user clicks ‘Sign in’, UC01 is performed, after which the system continues at step 2 of the basic flow without changing the experiment. **Alternative flow 2: The form is not complete** From step 4 of the basic flow, if no type is selected or the description is shorter than 20 or longer than 1000 characters, the system highlights the field and shows what is missing, for example “Please describe the problem in at least 20 characters.” Continue at step 3 of the basic flow. **Alternative flow 3: The report cannot be sent** From step 5 of the basic flow, if the connection is lost, the system displays “Your report could not be sent. Your text has been kept. Try again when you are online.” with the button ‘Try again’. **Alternative flow 4: The user cancels** From step 2 or 3 of the basic flow, the user clicks ‘Cancel’; the system closes the form and nothing is sent. **Alternative flow 5: The daily limit is reached** From step 3 of the basic flow, if the user has already sent 10 reports today, the system displays “You can send at most 10 reports per day. Please try again tomorrow.” **Alternative flow 6: The user reports an AI answer** When the form is opened from ‘Report this answer’ (UC05, alternative flow 8), the type ‘Wrong AI answer’ is preselected, and the question and answer are attached. Continue at step 3 of the basic flow.  |
| Pre-conditions | The user is signed in (or signs in through alternative flow 1). A guided experiment, an introduction page, a result summary, Open Lab or an AI answer is open. The computer is connected to the internet. |
| Post-conditions | A report with a unique number, the attached information and the status ‘Received’ is stored for review by Phy6, and the user has seen the report number. The experiment or Open Lab is unchanged. |
